    Order by a unique sort key, filter with a WHERE key > lastKey predicate, and take pageSize + 1 rows to know whether another page exists. Keyset paging stays fast on large tables because it avoids deep offsets.

    public sealed record Page<T>(IReadOnlyList<T> Items, int? NextKey);

public static class KeysetPaging
{
    public static async Task<Page<Order>> GetPageAsync(
        AppDbContext db, int? lastKey, int pageSize, CancellationToken ct)
    {
        var query = db.Orders.AsNoTracking().OrderBy(o => o.Id);
        if (lastKey is not null)
        {
            query = (IOrderedQueryable<Order>)query.Where(o => o.Id > lastKey);
        }

        var rows = await query.Take(pageSize + 1).ToListAsync(ct);
        var hasMore = rows.Count > pageSize;
        if (hasMore)
        {
            rows.RemoveAt(rows.Count - 1);
        }

        int? nextKey = hasMore ? rows[^1].Id : null;
        return new Page<Order>(rows, nextKey);
    }
}

    1008. How do I expose separate readiness and liveness health checks?

    Tag checks by purpose and map two endpoints with different tags, so an orchestrator can restart on liveness failures but only remove traffic on readiness failures. Keep liveness checks dependency-free.

    builder.Services.AddHealthChecks()
    .AddCheck("self", () => HealthCheckResult.Healthy(), tags: ["live"])
    .AddDbContextCheck<AppDbContext>("database", tags: ["ready"])
    .AddUrlGroup(new Uri(builder.Configuration["Dependencies:Payments"]!), "payments", tags: ["ready"]);

var app = builder.Build();

app.MapHealthChecks("/health/live", new HealthCheckOptions
{
    Predicate = check => check.Tags.Contains("live")
});

app.MapHealthChecks("/health/ready", new HealthCheckOptions
{
    Predicate = check => check.Tags.Contains("ready"),
    ResponseWriter = async (context, report) =>
    {
        context.Response.ContentType = "application/json";
        await context.Response.WriteAsJsonAsync(new { status = report.Status.ToString() });
    }
});

    1009. How do I call an external API through IHttpClientFactory with retries and a timeout?

    Register a named or typed client, attach a resilience handler for retries and a timeout, then inject the typed client. Never create HttpClient instances manually inside request handlers.

    public sealed class PaymentsClient(HttpClient http)
{
    public async Task<PaymentResult?> ChargeAsync(ChargeRequest request, CancellationToken ct)
    {
        var response = await http.PostAsJsonAsync("v1/charges", request, ct);
        response.EnsureSuccessStatusCode();
        return await response.Content.ReadFromJsonAsync<PaymentResult>(ct);
    }
}

// Program.cs
builder.Services.AddHttpClient<PaymentsClient>(client =>
    {
        client.BaseAddress = new Uri(builder.Configuration["Payments:BaseUrl"]!);
        client.Timeout = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(10);
    })
    .AddStandardResilienceHandler(options =>
    {
        options.Retry.MaxRetryAttempts = 3;
        options.Retry.BackoffType = DelayBackoffType.Exponential;
        options.AttemptTimeout.Timeout = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(5);
    });

    1010. How do I write a BackgroundService that shuts down gracefully on cancellation?

    Pass the stoppingToken into every awaited call so shutdown interrupts the loop promptly, and log exceptions without letting them kill the host. Wrap the loop body in a try/catch that respects cancellation.

    public sealed class OutboxPublisher(IServiceScopeFactory scopeFactory, ILogger<OutboxPublisher> logger)
    : BackgroundService
{
    protected override async Task ExecuteAsync(CancellationToken stoppingToken)
    {
        while (!stoppingToken.IsCancellationRequested)
        {
            try
            {
                using var scope = scopeFactory.CreateScope();
                var publisher = scope.ServiceProvider.GetRequiredService<IOutboxPublisher>();
                await publisher.PublishPendingAsync(stoppingToken);
            }
            catch (OperationCanceledException) when (stoppingToken.IsCancellationRequested)
            {
                break;
            }
            catch (Exception ex)
            {
                logger.LogError(ex, "Outbox publish cycle failed");
            }

            await Task.Delay(TimeSpan.FromSeconds(5), stoppingToken);
        }
    }
}

// Program.cs
builder.Services.AddHostedService<OutboxPublisher>();

    1011. How do I write an integration test that calls a real endpoint with WebApplicationFactory?

    Use WebApplicationFactory<Program> to boot the real pipeline in memory, then issue an HTTP request through the created client. Override services when you need to swap a real dependency such as the database.

    public sealed class OrdersApiTests(WebApplicationFactory<Program> factory)
    : IClassFixture<WebApplicationFactory<Program>>
{
    [Fact]
    public async Task Create_order_returns_created()
    {
        using var client = factory.CreateClient();
        var request = new CreateOrderRequest("ACME", 2);

        var response = await client.PostAsJsonAsync("/orders", request);

        Assert.Equal(HttpStatusCode.Created, response.StatusCode);
        var id = await response.Content.ReadFromJsonAsync<int>();
        Assert.True(id > 0);
    }
}

// Swap infrastructure in a derived factory when needed
public sealed class TestAppFactory : WebApplicationFactory<Program>
{
    protected override void ConfigureWebHost(IWebHostBuilder builder)
    {
        builder.ConfigureServices(services =>
            services.RemoveAll<DbContextOptions<AppDbContext>>());
    }
}
